<a id="shelf-1f6a3aec0a0e"></a>
### Like Engend’ring Like — Nicholas Russell

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 259.

History of heredity, livestock improvement and economic incentives, not an animal-breeding training manual. https://bahs.org.uk/AGHR/VOLUMES/AGHR35.pdf includes a specialist review identifying the sixteenth-to-eighteenth-century scope and integration of science, technology and economy. Source strongly endorses it, but endorsement is not independent proof. D=8 low confidence from review, explanatory structure and source report; contrasting breeding strategies and institutions add learning. Historical endpoint 1800 approximate; T11 England-centered history, no ecology penalty or T12. Original 1986.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-ac437943f6e6"></a>
### Urne Burial — Thomas Browne

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 259.

An antiquarian discovery prompts reflections on death, memory and impermanence. Its conceptual and literary payload passes V3, but difficult allusions reduce accessible learning and causal depth. The source stresses linguistic virtuosity rather than a reusable scientific model. Philosophy dominates; the meditations have no single historical endpoint.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-b83405b6ac94"></a>
### The Collapse of Complex Societies — Joseph A. Tainter

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 259, 263, 265.

Explains collapse through declining marginal returns to increasing organizational complexity. Comparisons expose resource costs and institutional constraints; the framework is useful but is not a universal law and causal identification is limited. Distinct cases justify substantial detail. Cross-period comparison has no single endpoint. https://books.google.com/books?id=M4H-02d9oE0C

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-9f3720e41b37"></a>
### Born to Rebel — Frank J. Sulloway

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 assessment of strong birth-order personality/rebellion claims. Large cross-national within/between-family study https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1506451112 found no broad personality effects outside intellect; https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2416709121 later reports some category/sibship associations, so no blanket assertion that birth order never matters. Neither supports the strong historical-rebellion explanatory framework. A smaller supportive conservatism study https://doi.org/10.1016/j.paid.2014.03.009 is also considered. V1 moderate for broad causal overreach. D=6.5 low-confidence description/research comparison: historical contrasts add material, repeated framework may offer less. No thematic deduction, H13=0 general developmental argument. Original 1996.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. The broad birth-order personality and historical-rebellion explanation exceeds support from stronger subsequent studies. Some later associations and smaller supportive studies are acknowledged; the veto is against causal overreach, not a claim of exactly zero possible birth-order effects. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-16433d26a20e"></a>
### Hereditary Genius — Francis Galton

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 assessment of an empirical historical argument, not a training/reference text. Original text https://galton.org/books/hereditary-genius/text/v5/galton-1869-hereditary-genius-v5.htm uses elite reputation and family concentration to infer inherited natural ability and extends this to racial hierarchy. Family environment, access and ascertainment are not separated sufficiently to support that central causal inference. General heritability of traits does not validate these specific estimates or racial conclusions. V1 high for this inferential failure, not based only on obsolete terminology or author reputation. D=5.5 low-confidence sampled contents/introduction estimate: occupational comparisons add data, long pedigrees often add little explanation. T11 predominant British elite and racial comparison; T12=0 heredity central. H13=0 general argument, publication deduction capped.

Veto V1; confidence high. The central inference from reputation and elite family concentration to inherited natural ability, extended to racial hierarchy, fails to separate shared environment, access and selection. A genuine heritable component to ability does not rescue these particular inferences. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-73ea141ef8ec"></a>
### A Troublesome Inheritance — Nicholas Wade

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 high: https://cehg.stanford.edu/letters-troublesome-inheritance records the cited field's researchers rejecting the claimed support for genetic explanations of institutional and economic differences. Source 259 also finds the promised genetic synthesis thin. The flaw is unsupported causal extrapolation from population variation, not the mere fact of variation or a politically unwelcome topic. D=5.5 moderate corroborated-review estimate: some genetics and historical summaries add information, recycled speculations dominate too much. Original 2014 cached OL19664185W. T11 racial history central; T12=0 genetics/human history rather than politics as primary subject. H13=0.

Veto V1; confidence high. Central genetic explanation of institutional and economic differences is not supported by the population-genetic evidence invoked; the researchers explicitly reject that inference. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-43dee9d1c224"></a>
### The Son Also Rises — Gregory Clark

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 moderate. https://doi.org/10.1177/0049124116661579 formally distinguishes surname-group persistence from individual persistence and shows the book's preferred inference is not identified without extra information. https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859015000061 credits the extensive surname evidence but critiques the general explanatory claims. Group persistence is real evidence; it does not by itself establish a universal individual mobility parameter or a genetic mechanism. D=7 low-confidence specialist-review estimate: cross-country records add comparisons, repeated universal inference adds less. Original 2014. T11/T12=0 social mobility across countries, not primarily finance or racial history. H13=0 cross-period argument.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. Surname-group persistence is used to infer general individual mobility without sufficient identifying assumptions; formal methodological criticism shows the measures are not interchangeable. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-ef8af22d5517"></a>
### Silicon Snake Oil — Clifford Stoll

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 moderate: broad dismissal extrapolates current limitations into enduring impossibility. Original 1995 and representative argument at https://archives.obs-us.com/obs/english/books/nn/bdstol02.htm dismiss recommendation agents partly because the author already has friends/reviews; source 259's rereading identifies useful short-run criticism but unjustified absolute predictions. A warning about educational costs remains valid independently, so the veto is not that every caution failed. D=6 moderate source-report/sample estimate: examples of technical limits add learning; repeated dismissals add less. T11/T12/H13=0 technology's usefulness central.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. Major enduring-impossibility judgments are extrapolated from temporary limitations and personal preference; some local cautions remain useful. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

Source 259 makes strong replication/publication-bias criticisms; these lower E, but its claim that multiple R-squared effects simply add toward 100% is not generally valid when predictors overlap. https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/cambridge-handbook-of-research-methods-and-statistics-for-the-social-and-behavioral-sciences/experimenter-effects/6CA55602C5D17C2E408D37944104E9FA confirms continuing relevance of experimenter bias, not validity of every older effect size. D=6 low-confidence sample/report estimate: study comparisons add distinctions, repeated small studies may yield less. No sufficiently corroborated book-wide V1 established. T11/T12/H13=0.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-e12c0d4efd2c"></a>
### Left In The Dark — Tony Wright and Graham Gynn

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V1 high. https://hermeticvision.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/12/Left-in-the-Dark.pdf directly links fruit diet correlations and speculative brain asymmetry to a species-wide deterioration thesis; the inference does not establish that causal chain. Publisher's revised-edition description https://www.innertraditions.com/books/return-to-the-brain-of-eden confirms the central lost-capacity/restoration claim. Original 2008 source 259, not unrelated Amanda Johnston poetry. D=3.5 moderate source-report/sample estimate: disconnected anecdotes dominate over explanatory evidence. T11/T12/H13=0 evolutionary consciousness argument, not environmental policy or a specific historical period.

Veto V1; confidence high. The central fruit-diet and hemispheric-degeneration causal account rests on unsupported extrapolation from correlations and anecdotes. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

### Reflections on Violence — Georges Sorel

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 259.

Counterfactual V6 high: prefaces, Class Struggle and Violence and Apology for Violence explicitly rally revolutionary syndicalist commitment through mobilizing myth and strikes. https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Reflections_on_Violence and https://files.libcom.org/files/Sorel-Reflections-on-Violence-ed-Jennings.pdf distinguish 1906 French serial under this title from revised 1908 book; use first production 1906. D=5 low-confidence sample/source estimate: distinctions between political and proletarian strikes add content, repetitive polemic reduces yield. T12 politics/philosophy, T11/H13=0 general mobilization argument.

Veto V6; confidence high. Representative chapters and prefaces chiefly rally revolutionary syndicalist allegiance through mobilizing myth and strikes. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.
